It scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any memories that you can't explain or gaps in your memory from the times when you were doing these things that didn't feel like you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are parts I can’t really account for. I remember some things almost too clearly, like the contractor’s face and the noise, but then there’s this chunk in the middle where I know I was there… I just can’t pull it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself unable to recall things that happened during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Often enough that it bothers me. I’ll remember certain parts really sharply… like his face, the noise. But then there’s this middle part that’s just missing. I know I was there, I just… can’t get it back.\n\nDoctor: Are these episodes of doing things that don't feel like you often triggered by something emotionally upsetting or stressful?\n\nPatient: Yeah… usually when I’m stressed. It’s like something builds up and then I just do something stupid, like leave during a deployment or drive around at night with no plan. That’s not how I used to handle things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about who you are or what's happening to you during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not exactly confused about who I am. More like I feel off, like I’m not fully tracking what’s happening. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have different parts of yourself that are in conflict with each other?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean… I argue with myself sometimes, but I don’t know about “different parts.” It’s more just feeling pulled in different directions, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there's more than one version of you, or that you're not always the same person?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think of it that way. I mean, I can feel different depending on what’s going on, but not like… more than one me. Not that I’ve noticed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s more like I realize afterward what I did, but I don’t feel like I’m watching myself from the outside.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your actions when you're doing these things that don't feel like you?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I’m physically doing it, so I guess I’m choosing it in the moment, but it doesn’t feel like me. Walking out during deployments, or driving around late with nowhere to go… afterward I think, why did I do that? It scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any memories that you can't explain or gaps in your memory from the times when you were doing these things that didn't feel like you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are parts I can’t really account for. I remember some things almost too clearly, like the contractor’s face and the noise, but then there’s this chunk in the middle where I know I was there… I just can’t pull it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself unable to recall things that happened during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Often enough that it bothers me. I’ll remember certain parts really sharply… like his face, the noise. But then there’s this middle part that’s just missing. I know I was there, I just… can’t get it back.\n\nDoctor: Are these episodes of doing things that don't feel like you often triggered by something emotionally upsetting or stressful?\n\nPatient: Yeah… usually when I’m stressed. It’s like something builds up and then I just do something stupid, like leave during a deployment or drive around at night with no plan. That’s not how I used to handle things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about who you are or what's happening to you during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not exactly confused about who I am. More like I feel off, like I’m not fully tracking what’s happening. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have different parts of yourself that are in conflict with each other?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean… I argue with myself sometimes, but I don’t know about “different parts.” It’s more just feeling pulled in different directions, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there's more than one version of you, or that you're not always the same person?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think of it that way. I mean, I can feel different depending on what’s going on, but not like… more than one me. Not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you forget things you did or learned before these episodes happened?\n\nPatient: Some of it, yeah. I remember certain parts really clearly… like the contractor’s face, and the noise. But there’s this part in the middle where I know I was there, and I just… can’t get it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel uncertain about your own identity or question who you really are?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I don’t sit around wondering who I am. I get confused about things sometimes, but not my identity, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of detachment from yourself or your surroundings when you're doing these things that don't feel like you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe a little… like things feel sort of distant sometimes. But I wouldn’t say I’m outside myself or anything. It’s hard to explain.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

It scares me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any memories that you can't explain or gaps in your memory from the times when you were doing these things that didn't feel like you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are parts I can’t really account for. I remember some things almost too clearly, like the contractor’s face and the noise, but then there’s this chunk in the middle where I know I was there… I just can’t pull it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself unable to recall things that happened during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Often enough that it bothers me. I’ll remember certain parts really sharply… like his face, the noise. But then there’s this middle part that’s just missing. I know I was there, I just… can’t get it back.\n\nDoctor: Are these episodes of doing things that don't feel like you often triggered by something emotionally upsetting or stressful?\n\nPatient: Yeah… usually when I’m stressed. It’s like something builds up and then I just do something stupid, like leave during a deployment or drive around at night with no plan. That’s not how I used to handle things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about who you are or what's happening to you during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not exactly confused about who I am. More like I feel off, like I’m not fully tracking what’s happening. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have different parts of yourself that are in conflict with each other?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean… I argue with myself sometimes, but I don’t know about “different parts.” It’s more just feeling pulled in different directions, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there's more than one version of you, or that you're not always the same person?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think of it that way. I mean, I can feel different depending on what’s going on, but not like… more than one me. Not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you forget things you did or learned before these episodes happened?\n\nPatient: Some of it, yeah. I remember certain parts really clearly… like the contractor’s face, and the noise. But there’s this part in the middle where I know I was there, and I just… can’t get it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel uncertain about your own identity or question who you really are?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I don’t sit around wondering who I am. I get confused about things sometimes, but not my identity, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of detachment from yourself or your surroundings when you're doing these things that don't feel like you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe a little… like things feel sort of distant sometimes. But I wouldn’t say I’m outside myself or anything. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience intense fear or anxiety when you realize what you've done during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes. When I realize I’ve done something like… leaving in the middle of a deployment, or driving around late at night with nowhere to go, it scares me. That’s not how I usually handle things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're spaced out or disconnected from the world around you when these things are happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Maybe a little, but I’m not really aware of it while it’s happening. It’s more like afterward I realize something was off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that there are periods of time in your life that you cannot remember, even when things were not particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: Not that I can think of. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have different parts of yourself that are in conflict with each other?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean… I argue with myself sometimes, but I don’t know about “different parts.” It’s more just feeling pulled in different directions, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there's more than one version of you, or that you're not always the same person?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think of it that way. I mean, I can feel different depending on what’s going on, but not like… more than one me. Not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you forget things you did or learned before these episodes happened?\n\nPatient: Some of it, yeah. I remember certain parts really clearly… like the contractor’s face, and the noise. But there’s this part in the middle where I know I was there, and I just… can’t get it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel uncertain about your own identity or question who you really are?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I don’t sit around wondering who I am. I get confused about things sometimes, but not my identity, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of detachment from yourself or your surroundings when you're doing these things that don't feel like you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe a little… like things feel sort of distant sometimes. But I wouldn’t say I’m outside myself or anything. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience intense fear or anxiety when you realize what you've done during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes. When I realize I’ve done something like… leaving in the middle of a deployment, or driving around late at night with nowhere to go, it scares me. That’s not how I usually handle things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're spaced out or disconnected from the world around you when these things are happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Maybe a little, but I’m not really aware of it while it’s happening. It’s more like afterward I realize something was off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that there are periods of time in your life that you cannot remember, even when things were not particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: Not that I can think of. I mean, I forget normal things sometimes, but I don’t think I have whole periods missing when nothing stressful was going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself discovering things you've done or said that you don't remember?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I haven’t noticed people telling me I said things I don’t remember. Maybe little things, but nothing that stands out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I’ll remember certain parts really sharply… like his face, the noise. But then there’s this middle part that’s just missing. I know I was there, I just… can’t get it back.\n\nDoctor: Are these episodes of doing things that don't feel like you often triggered by something emotionally upsetting or stressful?\n\nPatient: Yeah… usually when I’m stressed. It’s like something builds up and then I just do something stupid, like leave during a deployment or drive around at night with no plan. That’s not how I used to handle things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about who you are or what's happening to you during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not exactly confused about who I am. More like I feel off, like I’m not fully tracking what’s happening. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have different parts of yourself that are in conflict with each other?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean… I argue with myself sometimes, but I don’t know about “different parts.” It’s more just feeling pulled in different directions, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there's more than one version of you, or that you're not always the same person?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think of it that way. I mean, I can feel different depending on what’s going on, but not like… more than one me. Not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you forget things you did or learned before these episodes happened?\n\nPatient: Some of it, yeah. I remember certain parts really clearly… like the contractor’s face, and the noise. But there’s this part in the middle where I know I was there, and I just… can’t get it back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel uncertain about your own identity or question who you really are?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I don’t sit around wondering who I am. I get confused about things sometimes, but not my identity, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of detachment from yourself or your surroundings when you're doing these things that don't feel like you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe a little… like things feel sort of distant sometimes. But I wouldn’t say I’m outside myself or anything. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience intense fear or anxiety when you realize what you've done during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes. When I realize I’ve done something like… leaving in the middle of a deployment, or driving around late at night with nowhere to go, it scares me. That’s not how I usually handle things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're spaced out or disconnected from the world around you when these things are happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Maybe a little, but I’m not really aware of it while it’s happening. It’s more like afterward I realize something was off.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that there are periods of time in your life that you cannot remember, even when things were not particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: Not that I can think of. I mean, I forget normal things sometimes, but I don’t think I have whole periods missing when nothing stressful was going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself discovering things you've done or said that you don't remember?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I haven’t noticed people telling me I said things I don’t remember. Maybe little things, but nothing that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're disconnected from your thoughts or feelings when you're doing these things that don't feel like you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really think of it that way in the moment. It’s more like… I just don’t feel quite myself, but I can’t say I’m disconnected from my thoughts or feelings exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a dream-like state when you're doing these things that don't feel like you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not like a dream, exactly. Sometimes things feel… off, but I wouldn’t say dream-like.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control your actions during these episodes?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m totally unable to control myself. It’s more… I’ve been doing things that scare me after, like leaving in the middle of a deployment or driving around late at night with nowhere to go. It doesn’t feel like how I usually handle things.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience memory gaps for everyday events, not just the episodes where you did things that didn't feel like you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I forget normal things sometimes, but not… gaps, like losing chunks of the day. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
